# Ticket queue

Generated from docs/tickets.toml and the statuses in the work database by tools/tickets.py: never edit this file; read one ticket with `python tools/tickets.py show ID` and change tickets through the script. The ticket board shows the same data.

- Workers own one ticket or area for about a usage session, commit small green groups (all checks green), and follow docs/worker-brief.md. The coordinator merges, runs the suite and keeps this file current.
- A ticket goes running when its worker runs `work.py start`, review when the worker's branch is ready, done when it is merged (`work.py done ID merged`); statuses live in the work database, so every checkout sees them at once.
- Mechanical tickets go to Sonnet; type recovery, renderer and tricky debugging to Opus. Sonnet tickets change spelling only, never a signature or grouping; the first diff of each kind is reviewed before more are sent.

## Renderer (docs/notes/renderer.md)

Our own swap chain without dgVoodoo (renderer.md section 9), in parallel with the cleanup; one Opus ticket at a time.

- **R16i** GPU lighting and shadows (per-pixel sun and point lights, shadow maps) behind an option (review since 2026-10-22; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-22: no CPU lighting at all (asked if it is needed anywhere; digests carry no lighting value, lighting draws no rand). Light objects stay as GPU inputs. Done 2026-10-22 (review): no option, the GPU path only. Ground and figures lit per pixel (scene_lighting.hlsli, mesh.hlsl: the vertex lighting's and 3DfpFPU LightMesh's rules), sun and point lights; the ground casts into the sun map; R22 ids gone (N.L plus a 1.5-texel normal offset); classic shadows removed. Kept on the CPU (read outside drawing): SunReaches only where it would touch the sector cache, kLit / relight countdowns, the water specular word (digest), blood print levels (saved), AddSun's reference; sky lighting left on the CPU. render_shadows_outside's gate floor lowered to 0.3% (point lights cast no shadow); render_shadows_mound lost the facing A/B. Cost (render_stats 4K): main CPU zone1 4.46 -> 3.0-3.9, fight 15.4 -> 12.9, town 9.6 -> 5.3 ms; relit meshes 0; GPU within noise except town 2.1 -> 3.5 in one stretch. Frames build/R16i/compare_*.png. Image suite 121 the same, original 114 the same. Follow-up R16i1 (point-light shadows). Review fix 2026-10-22 (user: no unit shadows, the bz1g shop unshadowed at night): the towns' lamps (on by day too) lit the ground unshadowed over the units' shadows, now darkened by the same shade; meshes were shadowed only where their smoothed vertex normal faced the map's sun (the shop's plank read sideways), now every mesh by its face normal and its whole colour (as before R16i). The sun/moon map is drawn at night and the shop casts: not R16i1. bz1g noon/night against main: shadowed/lit brightness within 0.01. Crops build/R16i/unit_shadow_*_crop.png, shop_*_crop.png. Image suite 121 the same. Files: `src/render/`, `src/game/terrain_lighting.cpp`. After: R16.
- **R16i1** Point-light shadows (campfires, torches) in the GPU lighting (waiting on R16i; opus, 1 session). R16i lights the ground and figures per pixel by the sun and the point lights, but only the sun casts (one shadow map). In towns at night the fires now light the inside of object shadows (render_shadows_outside's gate: lighter where the fires reach; its floor lowered to 0.3%). Cube or dual-paraboloid shadow maps for the few strongest point lights in view (the campfire, torches carried by units), sampled in scene_lighting.hlsli's point loop and mesh.hlsl's figure_light. Measure the cost with render_stats (town at night); show the user before/after frames of bz1g at night (render_lighting's light_town_night). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13 item 9. Files: `src/render/render_shadows.cpp src/render/shaders/`.
- **R14** Option: stay on screen when focus is lost in borderless full screen (later; 1 session). User 2026-10-22: no UI for now; a settings-file flag in the Standalone key like R23's VSync (dword, default off, not read in the harness).
- **R15** Options: confine the cursor to the window; no edge pan with the cursor outside (later; 1 session). User 2026-10-22: no UI for now; a settings-file flag in the Standalone key like R23's VSync (dword, default off, not read in the harness).
- **R16j3** Shadow map: darkness where shadows overlap, and point lights (campfire) against the classic decals (later). Found in R16j1 (renderer.md section 13 item 10): no caster is missing; the user's 'missing' ruin and fern shadows are classic-decal effects. (1) Classic decals stack: overlapping objects each darken by half, the dirt under the fight's ruin is at 21% of its lit brightness classic and 43% with the map (kStrength 0.5, one sun). Options: a darker kStrength, or ambient-occlusion-like extra darkening where the map's blocker is close; show the user. (2) The classic direction sums the sun and the point lights in reach (0051CE60 LightDirection), so near the town's campfire shadows point away from the fire; the map has the sun only. Options: per point light a small cube or spot map for the strongest one or two lights near the camera (R16i territory), or leave. Wants the user's call; crops in build/R16j1. User 2026-10-21 on (1): 'Color-wise shadows looked about the same I think?' - keep kStrength 0.5, no extra darkening where shadows overlap. What is left is (2), point lights, which goes with R16i (GPU lighting). Files: `src/render/render_shadows.cpp`. After: R16j1.
- **R16e2** With the shadow map, the shadow sphere from the decal's box: no shadow projection (needs the user's call) (later; opus, 0.5 sessions). Found by R16e (renderer.md section 13 item 5). With the shadow map the object shadows' ProjectDecal 0056C750 (0.34 / 0.60 / 0.48-0.63 ms a frame in zone1 / the fight / the town, render_stats vanilla 4K) is read only for its mesh's bounds: the shadow's sphere (FitBounds in RenderShadow), which ShadowInView 0051E300 tests for objects out of view (CollectVisible 0056A990 then lists them for shadows and updates their pose) and vertices.size (DrawShadow's early return). A sphere around the decal's prism (the quad through the plane at the point minus depth * 0.33, clamped to the terrain's and platforms' heights under it) is conservative: more objects out of view listed for shadows (more casters in the map, which also helps the open 'casters off screen cast nothing'), more pose updates. Changes the shadow lists, so it waits for the user's call; check goldens 0 differ, render_shadows, renderstats' shadow projections gone with the map. Files: `src/game/map_object_shadow.cpp`, `src/game/world_client_decals.cpp`. After: R16e1.
- **R17** Visual upgrades plan: water, ambient occlusion, weather on surfaces, cloud shadows (later; opus). User 2026-10-21 asked to plan these for later: nicer water, ambient occlusion, weather that interacts with water and objects, cloud shadows on the ground. All are new-renderer features (src/render, sokol D3D11) behind options, off in the golden and capture runs so the suites keep comparing the classic look; each gets a frame-capture check of its own. Start after R16i (per-pixel sun and point lights), which most of them build on. Sub-tickets R17a-R17d; order to be picked with the user.
- **R17a** Nicer water: normal-mapped waves, Fresnel reflection of the sky, depth-based colour and shore foam (later; opus). Keeps R16c2's vertex waves and the game's water levels (height queries read them). Adds in the water pass: scrolling normal maps for small waves, Fresnel blend of a sky / environment reflection (screen-space reflection of objects as a later option), colour and opacity from the water depth (terrain height under the water page vs the water level, both already resident from R16c), foam where the depth goes to zero, sun specular from R16i's lighting. Fog as now. After: R16i.
- **R17b** Ambient occlusion: baked terrain AO plus screen-space AO for objects and units (later; opus). Two parts: (1) static AO per terrain page from the height field (computed once when a page goes resident, R16c), cheap and stable; (2) screen-space AO (GTAO or HBAO-style) from the depth buffer for meshes, figures and their contact with the ground, at reduced resolution with a bilateral blur. Applied to ambient light only (needs R16i's split of ambient and direct light). Option with quality levels. After: R16i.
- **R17c** Weather on surfaces: rain ripples and splashes on water, wet ground and objects, snow cover (later; opus). Driven by the game's weather state (weather_particles.cpp: rain / snow intensity, wind) without changing it: rain ripples (animated normal map) and splash particles on water pages, a wetness term (darker albedo, stronger specular, puddles in low terrain) building up and drying over time on ground and meshes, snow cover on up-facing surfaces (normal and height based) for snow maps. Purely visual: nothing in the simulation reads it. After: R17a.
- **R17d** Cloud shadows: a moving cloud mask projected from the sun onto ground and objects (later; sonnet). A tiling cloud noise texture scrolled with the wind, projected along the sun direction (the same projection as R16j's shadow map) and multiplied into direct sunlight for terrain, meshes and water. Density from the weather state (more cover in rain); off indoors / in caves and at night. Option, off in captures. After: R16j.
- **R19** Option: smooth world at high refresh rates (draw moving objects interpolated between ticks) (later; opus). Correction (user 2026-10-22): R19 is an EI_RENDER word like smooth / smoothcamera, not a settings-file flag; the flag note above is for R14 / R15 only. After: R18.
- **R24** Cache static outside casters' geometry on the GPU (later; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-22: later, not critical. R20's outside casters copy every part's world-space corners each frame (about +0.4 ms CPU in town, +1.2 ms with big buildings just off screen at 4K; 83k corners in the gate case). Static parts (trees, buildings) have the same world-space vertices every frame whatever the sun does: keep them in a GPU buffer per part, change only the sun's matrix per frame; the selection test stays per frame; animated parts and units keep the copy. Measure with the R21 overlay. After: R20.
- **R25a** Shadow map: calm the penumbra grain that changes as the sun turns (later). After R25 the shadow map's soft edges have no cell steps, but the per-pixel turned Vogel taps (8/16) make a grain whose pattern changes every frame as the sun turns the map's texels (render_shadows_shimmer, shadow view at 8 ms frames: 4-9% of the soldier's region by more than 8 of 255 between frames; real frame 2% by more than 3). Options: more taps where the radius is wide, reuse across frames, or a smoother prefiltered map. Only if the user still sees shimmer. After: R25.

- **R12a1** Smooth option: exercise the copy into the multisampled target (done 2026-10-21). R12a draws the world view multisampled and copies the frame target's colour and depth into every sample when a world run follows other draws in a frame (render_smooth.cpp CopyIntoMultisampled). No scenario reaches that order (the world view is the frame's first draw), so the copy is only compiled, never drawn. Add a harness way to reach it (e.g. a verify-build switch that puts a depth clear or blit before the world range) and check the frame against one without it.
- **R11d** Unit eye blink through the new renderer (done 2026-10-20). BlitMipChain (00533980, unit_client_blink.cpp) copies the face texture's open / closed eye rectangle onto the skin texture down the mip chain with DirectDraw blits; the skins are .mmp textures the new renderer decodes (no DirectDraw surface, no memory), so since R8 the blink copies nothing and party units never blink. Give the renderer a copy of a rectangle between two decoded textures on every mip level (render:: call keyed by the holders), called from BlitMipChain with the original's rectangles (units.md: closed top unscaled, open top scaled). Check: a scenario with a party unit near the camera and a frame capture of the face before and after a blink; goldens with party faces will change. After: R11c.
- **R16** Renderer architecture plan: draw from GPU-resident data instead of matching the old renderer's calls (done 2026-10-20; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-20: stop call-matching the old renderer; modernise it. Examples from the user: no texture blits (composite in shaders), no constant buffer re-uploads, decals on the GPU without re-uploads. Research ticket, no game-code changes: measure what a frame uploads and copies today (vertex / index / constant / texture bytes, blits, per-draw state changes, draw count) on a few scenes (zone1 outdoors, a town, a big fight, the interface), then write a short plan in docs/notes/renderer.md: static meshes, figures and terrain pages in persistent GPU buffers (skinning / morphs on the GPU), dynamic data through one ring buffer, instancing for map objects and particles, texture arrays or atlases, ground decals (prints, shadows, blood, spell marks) as GPU decals instead of re-baked textures, the interface and face / blink overlays composited in shaders, fewer passes. Each item: what it replaces, expected gain, how frame captures check it (tolerance, not bit-exact). End with tickets filed in order (R16a ...). Also a candidate (user asked how lighting works, 2026-10-20): today all lighting is CPU per-vertex (terrain_lighting.cpp: ambient + sun + heightfield ray shadows + point lights registered per vertex, relit on the LightingFreq countdown; figures lit per draw in 3DfpFPU slots 39-44; object shadows 64x64 CPU-rasterised textures on ground quads). Plan GPU lighting (sun + point lights per pixel, shadow maps or GPU-rendered shadow textures) with the game's vertex colours as the fallback; it changes the look, so it waits for the user's go-ahead. RenderDoc 1.43 is installed at E:\bin\renderdoc (x86 folder for the 32-bit standalone, in-app API renderdoc_app.h); usable for frame analysis behind an env var, captures under build\, no registry or RenderDoc settings changes (user 2026-10-20). Files: `docs/notes/renderer.md src/render/`.
- **R16a** Window surfaces by change marks and dirty-rectangle uploads, no memcmp per blit (done 2026-10-20; opus, 1 session). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13, item 1. Today every shown window surface is compared with its snapshot at every blit (4-32 MB a frame at 4K: 1-3 ms CPU on interface screens, about 0.9 ms in the world) and uploaded whole when it changed (up to 4 MB; the escape backdrop 32 MB). Mark changes where the game writes the memory (DC release 005FDC60, pixel code, SwapContents / Freeze, CopyFrameTo, greyscale; N0 census lists the writers), upload the changed rectangle. The harness build keeps the memcmp as a missed-writer check that fails the run. Measure with render_stats (renderstats: memory compared, CPU textures ms). Check: ui_* / render_* captures at 8 / 0.1%. Files: `src/render/render_textures.cpp`, `src/render/render_blit.cpp`, `src/game/ui_surface_dc.cpp`. After: R16.

Put the shadow textures in one texture array of slices updated with UpdateSubresource (mips on the GPU), mark changes from 0051CE60 TextureChanged instead of the snapshot compare, update other memory textures in place. Gain 0.5-1.5 ms CPU. Check: render_scene, render_options_shadows, ui_global_map_pick frames at 8 / 0.5%. R16j replaces object shadows by a shadow map (on by default); the shadow-texture part here only matters for the classic option, so R16b comes after R16j. Files: `src/render/render_textures.cpp`, `src/game/map_object_shadow.cpp`. After: R16.

Static position / uv / index buffers per sector page, LOD and seam variant; colours (8 of 28 bytes) in their own stream, uploaded when the sector was relit. Harness build: compare a cached page with a fresh build every N frames. Check: render_scene, render_terrain_seams, render_smooth with the cache on / off at 8 / 0.1%. Files: `src/render/render_terrain.cpp`. After: R16.
- **R16d** Meshes resident on the GPU: model-space corners kept, colours streamed (done 2026-10-20; opus, 1 session). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13, item 4. 0.3-2.3 MB of mesh vertices streamed a frame; 75% of meshes unchanged in zone1, most of the rest only relit. - **R16e** Ground decals (prints, blood, shadows, markers, light pools, spell marks) projected on the GPU (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). A GPU decal path takes under 0.07 ms in the default look (prints, markers), at most about 0.3 ms classic; the shadows' projection stays (its bounds are the shadow sphere ShadowInView 0051E300 reads). The real cost is the classic shadow build running with the shadow map: 0.72 / 1.40 / 2.1 ms a frame: follow-ups R16e1 (exact) and R16e2 (user's call). - **R16f** Present without the whole-frame copy: cursor and gamma ramp in the present pass (done 2026-10-21; sonnet, 1 session). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13, item 6. KeepFrameForCursor copies the whole frame every frame (33 MB of GPU traffic at 4K) before the cursor is drawn into it; draw the cursor in the present pass so the frame stays clean for the cursor thread. Check: render_cursor_present, render_present, render_present_shot (cursorcheck comparisons, 0 differences). - **R16g** Per-draw records in one ring buffer; instancing of meshes that share geometry (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13, item 7. After R16c/R16d: one ring buffer of per-draw records (matrix, fog, stage operations) instead of apply_uniforms per draw; instanced draws for meshes with the same geometry and state (132 of 350 in the fight), texture arrays where sizes match; materials from the game data instead of the stand-in device state. Particles only if a spell-heavy scene shows many pre-transformed draws (0-3 a frame measured). Check: render_scene, units at 8 / 0.1%.  Measured (R16g, renderer.md section 13 item 7; render_stats vanilla 4K, product-like build without sokol validation, per frame): all of DrawMeshBatch fight 0.30-0.40 ms (245-330 draws), zone1 0.17, town 0.15; of it uniforms 0.03-0.07 ms, pipeline + bindings 0.11-0.28, sg_draw 0.01; instancing could merge 47-77 fight draws (4 adjacent) at ~1.2 us each. Whole possible gain under 0.2 ms of the fight's 19 ms main-thread frame and nothing on the GPU: not built. Outcome: measured, not built (under 0.2 ms a frame possible; renderer.md section 13 item 7). Files: `src/render/render_mesh.cpp`. After: R16, R16d.
- **R16h** Figure animation (part morphs) on the GPU (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13, item 8. The game morphs figure positions on the CPU every frame; key frames on the GPU with the blend in the vertex shader remove the moved meshes uploads and the CPU morph. Bounds for picking and culling stay on the CPU. Check: models, units at 8 / 0.5%; goldens (picking). Outcome: measured, not built. Creatures animate by their bones (a matrix per part, already on the GPU); the per-vertex morph moves 0.6 of the fight's 331 mesh draws a frame (12 corners), none in zone1 / town / menu, no profile samples, and its result is read by game code (projection: visibility and screen rectangles; the shadow raster). Delivered: renderstats' morphed meshes and main-thread CPU / wall per frame; follow-ups R16k (device per-corner work) and SV10i (libcrt control word). Files: `src/render/render_mesh.cpp`, `src/game/figure_part_draw.cpp`. After: R16, R16d.
- **R16j** GPU shadows: units and objects cast shadows from the sun through a shadow map, replacing the CPU shadow textures (done 2026-10-20; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-20: 'we definitely want at least GPU shadows rendered in the modern way'; today's shadows are very inaccurate, especially on slopes, and update in a staggered way (64x64 textures rasterised and blurred on the CPU by 2DintMMX, map_object_shadow.cpp 0051CE60, rebuilt a few per frame, laid on flat ground quads by 0056C750). Render figures and map objects into a sun shadow map each frame (the sun direction from the terrain, TerrainFields +0x258 / SetSun; the meshes R16 already hands over with their matrices), sample it with filtering (PCF) when drawing terrain, objects and figures, so shadows fall correctly on slopes, platforms and other objects and move every frame. New shadows on by default in the image, the old ones kept as an option (EI_RENDER word or render:: switch, for ST2's options screen); with the option on 'classic', frames must match today at 8 / 0.1%. The game's own shadow building keeps running unless the goldens show it is visual only. Show the user side-by-side frames (frame_preview.py) of slopes, a town and a fight. Measure the CPU / GPU cost with render_stats. Terrain self-shadowing from the sun (CPU ray march in terrain_lighting.cpp) and per-pixel lighting stay in R16i. Files: `src/render/ src/game/map_object_shadow.cpp src/game/device_shadow.cpp docs/notes/renderer.md`. After: R16.
- **R16c2** Water waves in the terrain vertex shader (done 2026-10-21). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13 item 3. After R16c zone1 still uploads about 0.2 MB of terrain a frame, mostly its water pages: SectorData::Animate (006C3E80) moves every water vertex and sets the crests' specular on the CPU each tick. Base positions and the wave parameters (phase, direction, height, material priority and kind, the sine table) on the GPU and the lift in the vertex shader would leave only relit colours. Risk: float differences against the CPU waves (x87 sine table lookups); the game still reads heights on the CPU (HeightAt). Check: render_scene, terrain_water_load frames with the cache on / off; zone1 frames at 8 / 0.5%. Measured (R16c2): GPU waves not built, about 0.03 ms a frame to gain. The waves option is off in the harness (EnableWaterWaves 0): zone1's water uploads are animated tiles' texture coordinates (174 / 221 KB vanilla / Rebirth, writes 0.03-0.04 ms). With the option on Animate costs 1.0-1.2 ms in zone1, which stays on the CPU (the game reads the heights); GPU waves would take only the waves' share of the writes and the crests' specular. Built instead: TerrainChange (Rebuilt / Moved / WaterMoved), a page re-reads only for its own kind: zone1 34 -> 4.7 pages, 0.11 -> 0.05 ms a frame (Rebirth 0.13 -> 0.06); terraincache off now builds every page. Follow-up R16c3 (Animate's loop). After: R16c.
- **R16d2** Interface sprites kept on the GPU (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). docs/notes/renderer.md section 13 item 4. R16d streams interface sprites (CUiMesh::Draw 005E6D90, render::SetInterfaceDraw): the interface rewrites their quads and texels in place without RefreshBuffers (bars: ui_hud_panels.cpp, ui_hud_portraits.cpp; the minimap's markers and area: ui_hud_map.cpp; ui_hud_slide.cpp's markers; the original's and Rebirth's shape code). The global map uploads 106 + 75 KB of sprites a frame, the HUD a few KB. Signal each writer (render::MeshChanged Positions on the sprite) or keep a sprite's corners and compare its few source corners per draw, then drop SetInterfaceDraw. Check: meshcache check 0 stale in the ui_* and render_* scenarios; render_stats' map upload; frames with meshcache on / off at 8 / 0.1%. Measured, not built (2026-10-21): streamed sprites per frame (render_stats, vanilla 4K, temporary counter around their build): zone1 HUD 54 draws 17.9 + 6.1 KB 0.010 ms, fight 52 draws 18.6 + 6.1 KB 0.016 ms, camp 78 draws 30.5 + 14.5 KB 0.042 ms, global map 23 draws 105.8 + 74.9 KB 0.08-0.10 ms (+ about 0.02 ms ring copy), town and escape menu none. Keeping them still needs a per-draw read of the source corners: gain a part of 0.03-0.12 ms, nothing on the GPU, uploads small. SetInterfaceDraw stays (renderer.md section 13 item 4). Files: `src/render/render_mesh_cache.cpp`, `src/game/ui_mesh_draw.cpp`. After: R16d.
- **R16j1** Shadow map: map objects whose classic shadow shows cast none (the fight's ruin blocks) (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-20, on the fight preview (render_shadows' fight_map vs fight_classic, build/R16j/full/stone_full.png): 'Looks like the shadow is missing on this stone?'. At full 4K resolution the classic frame shows the ruin's front wall block, and the pillar to its left, throwing dark shadows down and to the right onto the dirt; with the map the dirt below them stays lit, while the figures do cast. Find out why: are those stones in the casters list (0051CE60 CastsShadow) and are their meshes attributed between BeginCaster / EndCaster (world_client_draw.cpp kDraw) or drawn elsewhere (sorted model batches, flat geometry, deferred), cut by kLeastStrength, alpha, the depth bias or the 160-unit caster reach? shadowcheck's counts and RenderDoc (E:\bin\renderdoc) on the map pass can tell. Also check the town and slope frames for the same. Fix so every object the classic shadows draw casts into the map; render_shadows passes, frames with the classic option unchanged, and show the user the same crop before / after. Second case, same day (town bz1g, town_map vs town_classic, build/R16j/full/bush_full.png): 'Here the bush shadow seems to be missing as well.' The small fern by the house's corner post casts a soft shadow down-left with classic shadows and none with the map (the house's own shadow shows), so check alpha-tested foliage too: stage 0's alpha cut in the caster pass (alpha reference, texture bound), first-pass-only, and whether bushes reach CastsShadow. Files: `src/render/render_shadows.cpp src/game/world_client_draw.cpp src/game/map_object_shadow.cpp`. After: R16j.
- **R16j2** Shadow map: soft edges like the old shadows (wider near the far end, sharp at contact) (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-20: 'the new shadows are quite a bit harder than the original one, see the shadow of the town gate'. The old shadows are 64 x 64 textures stretched over each object's shadow quad and, with the shadow option on 'blurred' (mode 2, 0079BD0C), blurred twice on the CPU (2DintMMX BlurShadow / BlurShadow2, map_object_shadow.cpp ~358): a penumbra of a few texels of a 64-texel image, so about a tenth of the object's size, wide on a building. The map is 4096 texels over the visible ground (about 72 units) with 3 x 3 PCF, an edge of a few hundredths of a unit. Make the edge soft: contact-hardening (PCSS: blocker search, penumbra from the receiver's distance to the blocker times the sun's chosen angular size, Poisson-disc taps, rotated per pixel) so a shadow is sharp where an object meets the ground and soft away from it; tune the largest penumbra to look like the old one on the town gate (render_shadows' town_map vs town_classic). Keep the classic option unchanged, and the GPU cost of the map pass + sampling within about 0.3 ms at 4K (render_stats). Show the user the town gate, the fight and a slope before / after. Files: `src/render/render_shadows.cpp`. After: R16j1.
- **SV10e** Harness build's renderer read-backs (CheckUpload, gamma ramp check) sampled instead of every time (done 2026-10-21; sonnet, 0.5 sessions). SV10 profile (render_stats, image): the verify build's CheckUpload (render_blit.cpp: every cursor / fill / movie image upload read back from the GPU and compared, with ReadPassState) is ~3% of the main thread, a GPU stall each time; the gamma ramp check reads the presented frame back after each ramp change. Check the first N uploads of each kind and then every 16th (and every one in scenarios that ask, e.g. render_* ones), so a mismatch still shows within a run. No golden reads them (the digest is game state, captures read the frame through their own path); keep the log's counts. Gain ~3% of draw-heavy runs. Files: `src/render/render_blit.cpp src/render/render_window.cpp`.
- **R16k** Device mesh draws without the old path's per-corner work (TL vertices, clipping, fog) when the new renderer takes the mesh (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). Found by R16h (profile of render_stats, image): DrawMesh 004E9010 / 004E9A50 (device_mesh.cpp) still fills a TL vertex per corner (FillCorners), clips meshes crossing the view (ClipTriangles, ToScreenClipped), fogs them (FogVertices) and submits them for every pass, though the new renderer draws every pass from the model-space hand-over (MeshPass takes it; the old path draws nothing). Game code reads only the projection's verdict and, with draw flag 1, the screen rectangle (BoundsOfProjected; for a crossing mesh BoundsOfVertices of the clipped vertices: keep the clip for flag 1 only), and the device's counters (vertexCount_, primitiveCount_, drawCount_). Skip the rest when render::Active(), keeping the passes' hand-over (DrawIndexed per pass with the same counts). Measure first: render_stats' fight and town main-thread CPU per frame (renderstats' new field) with and without. Check: goldens (picking reads the rectangles), render_scene / models / units samecapture A/B at 8 / 0.1%. Files: `src/game/device_mesh.cpp src/game/device_draw.cpp`.
- **R16e1** With the shadow map, skip the classic shadow's raster, blur, texture copy and decal draw (read by nothing) (done 2026-10-21; opus, 0.5 sessions). Found by R16e (renderer.md section 13 item 5). With the shadow map (the default) DrawShadow 0051CE60 still builds the classic shadow every frame for moving objects: RenderShadow rasterises the parts into the 64 x 64 texture (CLinkMesh::RasterisePartImpl / RasteriseFigureMesh, BlurShadow / BlurShadow2, the lock and copy into the render surface, LoadPixelsFrom, render::TexturePixels), and DrawFlatBlocks 004EA4A0 projects and clips the decal, which BeginMesh then drops. With the map the game reads only the parts' points in the light's view (self->bounds: the quad), the projected decal (ProjectDecal: the sphere ShadowInView 0051E300 tests, vertices.size) and nothing of the texture. Skip the raster, blur, copy and the decal's flat draw when the new renderer is active and the shadows are the map's; keep the points, bounds, quad, projection and sphere (the same game state). A switch to classic must build every shadow again (kFlagRefresh on each, or a stale mark: objects with flags 0x600 are not rebuilt every frame). Gain (R16e, render_stats vanilla 4K, renderstats 'shadow builds' + 'flat draws: shadows'): zone1 0.31 + 0.065, fight 0.73 + 0.072, town 1.15-1.30 + 0.17-0.21 ms a frame (part of the build is the points' projection, which stays). Check: those timers before / after, render_shadows (classic captured after the map: rebuilt textures; classic against remade at 8 / 0.5%), render_options_shadows, goldens 0 differ. Files: `src/game/map_object_shadow.cpp`, `src/render/render_shadows.cpp`.
- **R16c3** Water waves' CPU loop (Animate) faster, bit-exact (done 2026-10-21). R16c2 measured SectorData::Animate 006C3E80 at 1.0 ms a frame in zone1 (1.2 Rebirth, 0.35 in the town) with the water waves option on (EnableWaterWaves; off in the harness's registry, so no scenario reaches the waves now). The circling waves' sineX / sineY depend only on the column / row and the world time (read per vertex): 33 values each per call; the time once per call. Same float expressions, so bit-exact; needs a scenario with the option on (a harness step setting enableWaterWaves_ before newgame) and a golden or vectors for Animate. docs/notes/renderer.md section 13 item 3 (R16c2).
- **R16c4** Inlined FISTP for Animate's per-vertex rounding (done 2026-10-21; opus). R16c3 (2026-10-21): each water vertex in SectorData::Animate 006C3E80 still makes up to four out-of-line x87::RoundToInt calls (one sine lookup, three colour bytes). An inlinable FISTP in include/ei/x87.h (same instruction, same control word, so bit-exact) would cut Animate further; check no other caller changes beyond the call, measure Animate A/B with waterwaves 1 (stop under 0.1 ms a harness frame). Also record terrain_water_waves's Rebirth golden (only vanilla exists). Checks: goldens both installs, terrain_water_waves the same. After: R16c3.

Five folds through inlined constant helpers fixed (HUD clock x2, portraits, unit panel, cylinder particles); addon's 0.001 allow-listed. - **R9b3** Dead old-device and game.exe code-byte paths out (done 2026-10-21; sonnet). R9b2 (2026-10-21) leftovers: the dead game.exe code-byte reads (app_frame.cpp 004580D7, script_parse 004CF69B; game.exe runs only the original since S21), the vertex-buffer batch code in device_binds.cpp / device_object.cpp (no batch ever gets a vertex buffer; DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB at device_binds.cpp:32 would now end the run), and device.h's interface-forwarding branch if only the harness uses it (keep what game.exe runs need). Checks: goldens both installs, the original suite, build checks at 0. After: R9b2.
- **R9b4** FlushBatch calls out (done 2026-10-21). R9b3 (2026-10-21) left FlushBatch / FlushBatch2 (004E7840 / 004E7A40) as empty ports and UiRenderer::FlushBatchA/B: remove them with their ~35 call sites in renderer_*, device_draw, ui_hud, ui_alpha_draws, ui_container_draws, ui_hero_preview and world_client_draw once C11's shim batches have merged (the Flush helpers in the ui_ and world_client_draw files go with them), then dead_code.csv rows for both addresses. After: R9b3.
- **R18** Option: smooth camera at high refresh rates (no per-tick camera jumps, time-based easing, interpolated follow) (done 2026-10-21; opus). User 2026-10-21: the camera moves jerkily at 1x speed on a 120 Hz screen. Cause: world ticks are 55 ms (0078FF34); the main loop moves the camera once per frame by the elapsed real time (RunPass, app_main.cpp) and again by a whole tick in RunWorlds on every client tick, so about half of each pan / turn / zoom arrives as a jump 18 times a second. CCamera::Update's tracking drift (kTrackGain 0.2, 0073BAD4) and focus height lag (kHeightLag 0.1) are per call, not per second, so they change with the frame rate. A followed unit's position only changes per tick in single player. Behind an option, off by default (goldens unchanged): drop the per-tick camera update and give the per-frame update the same total time so speeds stay as they are; make the two gains time-based (1 - (1 - g)^(dt / reference)); follow the tracked unit's position blended between its last two ticks by the client's tickFraction_. Check with a scenario at a fixed frame time that the camera covers the same distance as without the option. Done 2026-10-21: option smoothcamera (EI_RENDER word / render::SetSmoothCamera, off by default; renderer.md section 14). On: no camera update at client ticks, the frame's update gets real time + the client time that passed (own clock: ticks run + backlog up to a tick), drift/height lag 1-(1-g)^(dt/ref) with ref = one call's time at 60 fps (0.465 ticks), target blended between its last two ticks by tickFraction_ (camera::NoteTargetTick). Check: scenario camera_smooth (image only, frametime 8 on the virtual clock): pan/turn/zoom totals within 2.0/0.4/0.2% of the plain camera's, 0 jumps with the option (23/20/16 without), follow 0 jumps. Suites: image 121 the same, original 114 the same. For R19: the followed place lags the unit by up to a tick; units still draw at tick positions, so a followed unit steps on screen until R19 interpolates the draws. Files: `src/game/app_main.cpp`, `src/game/camera.cpp`.
- **UB1** Chat window's face and status vanish depending on the game heap's layout (done 2026-10-21; opus). Found by U1 (2026-10-21): in ui_gallery_mp's briefing / chat_typed / chat_sent frames the base screen's player list loses its face and status (top right, 27249 pixels) when the game heap shifts. Reproduced with the UTF-8 switch off by leaking gamecrt::operator_new(16/64/100) on every other text lookup; game state digests the same. Guess: a window texture or canvas keyed by holder address in render_textures / render_ui is reused or stale. Fix it so the frames do not depend on heap layout; it otherwise shows in every U ticket's frame check and can make goldens flaky. Done (2026-10-21): not the renderer. Connection::SendUpdate writes a priority's entries in the order of their ReplicaEntry addresses (priorities_ keyed by pointer), so the player record can reach the client before its binding; ShowPlayers lists nobody without the binding and the original's binding handler 0041E430 does not refresh: face and status gone. Fix as the T2f address orders: with address_order::ById the binding refreshes the list (OnPlayersChanged(1)) when players came first; the harness patches the original's 0041E430 to a stub doing the same. Regression check: scenario step checkplayers (ui_gallery_mp) fails when the list is short; run with EI_HEAP_JITTER. Before: image failed seeds 2,fill and 4, the original even plain in this build; after: image plain/1-6 and original plain/1/2,fill/3-6 pass, frames pixel-identical to main's plain frames. Suites: image 121 same, original 114 same. Files: `src/render/render_textures.cpp`, `src/render/render_ui.cpp`.
- **R20** Shadows of objects outside the view (shadow pop at the screen edge) (done 2026-10-22; opus, 1 session). Done 2026-10-22 (worktree-agent-aa161a03c455d1853): the world's draw walks the object grid's sectors over the visible ground swept back towards the sun and hands the shadow map every object (or undrawn part) whose light-space bounds overlap the map's area on the sun's side of the ground's far depth (exact up to the bounding boxes; no distance rule); the map's depth starts at the nearest such caster (160-unit room gone; caster shader pancakes). Read-only on the game's objects; goldens 121 / 114 the same. Scenario render_shadows_outside (walked frames match the game's draws; moved-town gate shadow present, 1.8% of the frame). Cost at 4K: map CPU +0.05 ms (zone1 start), +0.4 (town), +1.0 (town gate, 83k corners outside); GPU within noise.
- **R21** Performance overlay in the standalone, switched by the settings file (done 2026-10-22; opus, 1 session). Done 2026-10-22: render_perf.{h,cpp} + shaders/perf_overlay.hlsl; switch [Software\Nival Interactive\EvilIslands\Standalone] "PerfOverlay"=dword:1 in ei_settings.ini (directdraw_holder.cpp, not read in the harness build); shows FPS, frame avg/worst, GPU shadow/world/UI/present (timestamps), CPU submit/shadow, draws; drawn into the back buffer only. Checked by ei_perf_check (WARP, build.cmd). renderer.md section 15.
- **R22** Shadow map: hard cutoffs on terrain mounds (done 2026-10-22; opus, 1 session). Done 2026-10-22: cause: R16j's receiver rule left faces turned away from the sun (face normal from derivatives) unshadowed; the game lights objects like the ground, not by sun direction, so inside another object's shadow those faces stayed lit with straight edges (the mound is a flora object; reproduced only with flora shadows on, the user's Rebirth setting). Fix: casters write their object's id beside the map (R16UI); a receiver's own object shadows only faces turned to the sun, any other object every face (per-tap ids where facing < 1). Scenario render_shadows_mound (user's save, harness 'shadowfacing all|others' A/B). Before/after: build/R22/after1.png, cmp_fight.png, cmp_town_gate.png. After: R20.
- **R23** Vsync switch in the settings file (done 2026-10-22; sonnet, 1 session). Implemented: settings file Standalone key VSync (dword, 0 = off, default on). Off: sync interval 0, and where IDXGIFactory5 supports it the flip chain is made with ALLOW_TEARING (ResizeBuffers same flags) and presents carry ALLOW_TEARING; both present paths (render_window.cpp, render_cursor.cpp). Rules in render_present_mode.cpp, checked by ei_perf_check (vsync part, hidden-window chains on WARP). Harness never reads it (VsyncSwitchSupported false); harness SetWaitForVsync(false) unchanged, no tearing flags. Docs: renderer.md 15a, play.cmd.
- **R25** Shadow map: shimmering inside shadows at some camera distances (done 2026-10-22; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-22: the save holds the exact camera distance; no zoom needed. Zooming in removes it, zooming back out brings it back. Reproduce with a still camera over consecutive frames and diff them inside the triangle. Done: cause = receivers read the depth-range image per 16-texel cell (radius bound, early ends, search stepped at cell edges: squares and straight cuts in penumbras), and the sun turns the map's texels every frame so the squares crawled. Fix: range sampled bilinearly between 4 cells, dilation +1 cell, search reads filtered, searched radius blended in from half a cell. Harness shadowview/shadowshift; scenario render_shadows_shimmer (save tests/saves/shadow_corrupt). Before/after: build/R25/compare_*.png. Open: the turned taps' grain still changes as the sun turns (small). After: R22.

## WideScreen (docs/notes/rebirth.md)

Rebirth's WideScreen patch folded in (100 sites), one Opus ticket at a time; `run_all --widescreen` compares with addon.

- **W4c** EI_Plugin NPlayers above 7: AddPortrait's six sites (later; sonnet, 0.25 sessions). From W4b: AddPortrait (00640060) holds six sites active only when EI_Plugin.ini NPlayers > 7; not reproduced. A Rebirth launch with NPlayers > 7 keeps the original there (rebirth_hook Check skips the function); the standalone treats NPlayers > 7 as 7 in the portraits. Port if anyone plays with more than 7.
- **W1** Layout widths, scaling functions, harness resolution steps (done 2026-10-14). Widescreen layout widths, scaling functions (SetRect / AddPoint / sprite place) and the `resolution` / `widescreen` harness steps; run_all --widescreen within tolerance.
- **W2** Sprite shapes, text extent and text draw, control rectangles (done 2026-10-14; opus). Widescreen sprite shape builders, text extent / text draw and the +0x98 control rectangles (console / edit / list / chat / tooltip). After: W1.
- **W3** Battle-screen panel builders (done 2026-10-14; opus). Widescreen battle-screen panel builders; widescreen::SetPanelOffset replaces addon's variable. After: W2.
- **W4** Camp and hero screens, help, global map, cursor rectangles, interface creation (done 2026-10-15; opus). Widescreen camp and hero screens, help, global map, cursor rectangles and interface creation (also camera mouse moves and 004F1330). After: W3.
- **W2a** Connect list 0061D780 under Rebirth: ExtendedServerName with WideScreen (done 2026-10-14; sonnet, 0.5 sessions). Connect list 0061D780 reproduces ExtendedServerName (+0x27C) with WideScreen under Rebirth (EI_REBIRTH_IMPLEMENTS both). After: W2.
- **W2b** Connect screen Create 0061B2C0: addon's ExtendedServerName sites (done 2026-10-15; sonnet, 0.3 sessions). Connect screen Create 0061B2C0 reproduces addon's ExtendedServerName sites (field sizes, 0061BB51 and neighbours, 0061B5CE float), gated on Patch::ExtendedServerName. Files: `src/game/ui_connect_screen.cpp`, `config/rebirth_patches.csv`.
- **W4a** Hero screen 00613EA0 under Rebirth: EiPluginFixes with WideScreen (done 2026-10-15; sonnet, 0.5 sessions). Hero screen 00613EA0 reproduces EI_Plugin's EiPluginFixes sites (+D15 / 00614BB5, +E7B / 00614D1B) behind their flag under Rebirth (EI_REBIRTH_IMPLEMENTS both). After: W4.
- **W4b** EI_Plugin's remaining Fixes and NPlayers sites (done 2026-10-15; sonnet, 0.5 sessions). EI_Plugin's remaining EiPluginFixes / EiPluginNPlayers sites (005ED7B0, 006091C0, 006407E0, 005F13A0, 00536100, 00640060, 00608D20) reproduced behind the flag; ei::rebirth::PluginNPlayers().
- **W5** Dialogue window: two overlapping frames (done 2026-10-22; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-22, playing play.cmd (Rebirth data, Rebirth profile with WideScreen=1, the starter's WideScreen 1, 2703x1777 window, UTF-8 build): the dialogue window shows an outer frame and an inner frame offset from it (inner starts further right and lower), text drawn from the outer one. Likely WideScreen (Rebirth's patch folded in, or ui_widescreen) moving or scaling one of the dialogue's panels twice or not at all. Reproduce in a harness scenario (dialogue on the Rebirth install with widescreen), compare with addon's drawing (run_all --widescreen) and fix. Cause: CBriefingScreen::Update (00604130) showed every mesh in the phrases / rewards states; the original shows meshes 2 onwards, so the topics' frame (mesh 1, 195..605 x 425..575) was drawn inside the text's frame. Not WideScreen: every resolution and canvas (main's ui_gallery_base base_phrase showed it at 16:9 without the flag). Fixed in ui_briefing_create.cpp; ui_widescreen_dialogue compares the phrase with addon's recorded frame (fixed: 0 px differ; unfixed: fails), Rebirth golden.

## Unknown fields

Every unknown_XXX member becomes a real name or unused_XXX (same type and offset; renames only, layout unchanged). check_cleanup counts `unknown field` (727 in 97 files on 2026-10-14) and `unused field accessed` (stays 0). Rebirth's DLL may read a field we call unused.

- **F3b** Interface typed views after F3a (later; sonnet, 0.3 sessions). From F3a (2026-10-16): the HUD reaches the message log as children_.data[9] (ui_battle_commands, ui_battle_setup, ui_hud_panels, ui_hud_top_panel) instead of CBattleScreen::messageLog_; the battle tests view CBattleScreen::party_ as a UiDwordArray; ui_briefing.h's party is still a UiDwordArray; CUiWindow repeats CTransformObject's first 0x54 bytes (derive if the vtable and constructor allow).
- **F1** Unknown fields: world, combat, effects, spells (done 2026-10-14). Unknown fields in world, combat, effects and spells named or marked unused_XXX (181); meanings in cleanup-world.md / cleanup-effects.md. Files: `world.h`, `combat.h`, `spell.h`, `effects.h`, `world_effect.h`, `sky_object.h`, `lightning / trap / lever / cage`, `particle_emitters.cpp`.
- **F2** Unknown fields: units, AI, items (done 2026-10-14). Unknown fields in units, AI and items named or marked unused_XXX (172); meanings in cleanup-units.md. Files: `unit.h`, `unit_db.h`, `unit_ai.h`, `ai_map.h`, `ai_*`, `item_db.h`, `item*`, `unit_looks.h`, `unit_client_*`, `class_templates.h`, `party*`.
- **F3** Unknown fields: interface (~125) (done 2026-10-15). Unknown interface fields (~125) renamed, coordinated with W2 (ui.h) and the P tickets. Files: `ui.h`, `ui_camp.h`, `ui_screens.h`, `ui_briefing.h`, `ui_*`.
- **F4** Unknown fields: scene, figures, terrain, map objects, textures (~140) (done 2026-10-14). Unknown fields in scene, figures, terrain, map objects and textures: 143 named (90) or unused_XXX (53); meanings in cleanup-figures.md (F4 section). Files: `scene_loader.*`, `map_object_figures.h`, `terrain*`, `figure*`, `camera.h`, `texture.h`, `map_object*`, `link_figure.h`, `object_tables.h`, `material.h`, `model.h`, `renderer_lines.h`.
- **F5** Unknown fields: the rest (~105) (done 2026-10-14). Files: `map_spec.h`, `net_*`, `player_*`, `sound*`, `script*`, `app_*`, `bink_video.cpp`, `quest_records.h`, `base_manager.h`, `src/lib`.

## Standalone verification (docs/notes/standalone-verification.md)

The SV loop comes before any standalone ticket (user 2026-10-14).

- **SV2c** unit_create parted from its golden once in crt.rand (watch). Moved to watch 2026-10-15: one parting under load in SV2a's era; not seen in today's main suites. After: SV2a.
- **SV6c** Startup crash inside AIL_waveOutOpen (EIP 00007EBC), seen once (watch). SV6b's full suite, zone_gz15h_tornado-vanilla first attempt: C0000005 at 00007EBC on the main thread inside AIL_waveOutOpen (harness MutedWaveOutOpen <- CSoundManager::Open via SoundOpenVerify), before the menu; retry passed. Watch main suites for a repeat (exception at a low address with MutedWaveOutOpen in the stack) before chasing it.
- **SV7b5** Net replication ordered by pointer (later). T2f (merged) already orders DiffObjectSets/SyncPlayer object sends (0047ADF0, 0047B4C0) by object id on both sides; the Connection maps here are the remaining part. After: SV7a.
- **SV4c1** Path vectors on more maps: smaller AI map snapshots (later). SV4c keeps 3 of 18 recorded maps (888 KB; zone1 alone 607 KB, gz3g 1.2 MB, the menu map 80 KB per run). A snapshot coded against a base map of the same size, or a small inflater in ei_vectors (zlib halves the planes), would let gz3g, bz1g and gz2g's searches in. tools/path_vectors.py prune --keep chooses the maps. After: SV4c.
- **SV5a1** TryTame and the AI's charm: a caster with enough mana (later; 1 session). SV5a: CUnitServer::TryTame (00551AE0) stays unreached. The hero's 56 mana is far below charm's cost and the zone wolves never pick charm. Needs a script-raised caster (GiveIntelligence / GiveSkill astral, high mana) and a Cast order of charm at a unit, or an AI unit with charm among its spells; scratch tries are spells_charm and unit_ai_charm in standalone-verification.md SV5a. After: SV5a.
- **SV5a2** Hook-reach for AI hooks our code calls directly (LookAroundHook, SeeCorpseHook, AICallArray::Add, SuspectionMode) (later; 1 session). SV5a: SuspectionMode::Execute/Enter/Leave, PatrolPointArray::Add/SetAtGrow, LookAroundHook (005CFE40), SeeCorpseHook (005CBE90), AICallArray::Add (005CD880) are unreached. SeeCorpse logic runs but our code calls the hook functions directly, so the cover INT3 on the game address never fires; decide whether coverage should count ours-to-ours calls via the first byte of our function, or a scenario with a controlled party member dying (AddUnitUnderControl trips the CUnit::SetPlayer verify wrapper: ours-only PlayerOwnsUnit check). CTrapObject::UpdateTrap needs a trap object with a category-2 consumable (none found). After: SV5a.
- **SV5a3** Verify wrapper for UseOnUnit with a potion (synchronously consumed) (later; 1 session). SV5a: a quick-item potion (category 8) used on a unit fails the CBattleScreenSetup::UseOnUnit wrapper because the potion is consumed at once and the member does not nod (ours-vs-original mismatch). Make the wrapper tolerant or compare after the consume, then add a potion scenario (ui_quick_potion was dropped); also UseOnUnitVerify / SetPlayerVerify (ours-only PlayerOwnsUnit check) reject AddUnitUnderControl. After: SV5a.
- **SV7c1** Vanilla menu unit cunit2 not hidden (flags 8) once under EI_HEAP_JITTER=3 (later; opus, 1 session). SV7c, one-off: vanilla --no-hooks EI_HEAP_JITTER=3 ui_saveload, record 0 (update 20, main menu): cunit2 unmoco2 flags 0x100004A2 where the golden has 0x100004AA (bit 8, not drawn, which UpdateClient 005341A0 sets at update 4 from the current animation's key: a still idle). Same isaac and crt.rand state, same place, same animation entries. Did not repeat in three fresh j3 reruns, one with EI_WATCH_CUNIT=2,flags, nor in any other of ~3500 jitter / fill runs. Candidate: which menu unit takes which IsaacRandom draw in AdvanceTime 00535B30's long first step (500..600), so the idle's phase at update 4; or a timing effect. Chase with EI_WATCH_CUNIT=2,flags over many runs if it shows again. Files: `src/game/unit_client_update.cpp`, `src/game/unit_client_animation.cpp`, `tests/verify/harness_watch.h`.
- **SV5d6** Streamed music on the virtual clock: the music system's draws follow the real-time stream thread (later; opus, 1 session). SV5d5 found net_pair_quest's flake (C13b3) on the original and the image alike: UpdateMusic 006B5350 picks a fight's combat track (PickName, a GlobalRandom draw) only when CurrentStreamName 006BAEA0 does not report it playing, and the calm branch runs only while nothing plays; the stream thread (006BB910) sets that state as it plays, in real time, so the draw count followed the machine's load. net_pair_quest now plays with 'music 0' (AudioStreaming answered 0). Every other scenario with fights and streamed music keeps the exposure. Make the stream state the main thread sees follow the virtual clock in the harness (both the original and the image), e.g. the stream thread's progress and fades advanced from the main loop as harness_clock does for sample ends; re-record only the goldens it changes (docs/notes/sound.md, Non-determinism). Files: `tests/verify/harness_clock.cpp src/game/sound_music.cpp src/game/sound_manager_lifetime.cpp`. After: SV5d5.

- **SV5d5** net_pair_quest takes 130-240 s a run: find where the time goes and shorten it (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-20 asked whether net_pair_quest's run time had been improved: it has not. work.db's run table: 30 s before SV5d3 added the quest, then 130-240 s every run (standalone 133-238, vanilla 143-214; two games each, timeout 480 against the 60 s default), the longest scenario in both suites. The time goes to: the hero walking across zone9 to the old camp and back (lookat + clickground steps repeated because fights stop the walk, frames 150 each), the ten undead's fights, the briefing clicked through with wait 4000 / 800 steps (twice), and the 60 s of game time before CompleteZone's awards reach the clients (frames 400). Measure each part from the run's ei_hook.log timestamps first, then shorten without losing what the scenario reaches (CompleteZone 00418ED0, kind 0 / 1 awards, ClientOnAward 0041EF90, OnMaster 0041ACA0): e.g. the game's own speed-up (harness speed / the virtual clock if the pair can run on it), fewer and shorter walk steps, waitwindow instead of fixed waits. Only inputs the original handles. Goldens re-recorded only if the steps change; vanilla and image goldens must still agree. It also flakes on the original: C13b3 (2026-10-20) saw net_pair_quest vanilla's golden differ once (random count 233 against 222 at tick 19472; two fresh reruns the same), and R16d reran it on the original again, so workers spend extra 3-minute runs confirming a flake on code they never touched. Find the timing that moves the random count in the two-process pair and make the scenario deterministic (or wait on an event instead of a frame count there). Done (SV5d5): 6400 frames -> 2840 (vanilla 61-69 s, the image 72-89 s under load, from 130-240 s): walks wait on waitidle (20 idle frames; 3800 old frames were the hero idle in fixed frames 150), the camp on waitdead, the end on waitaward (the award reaches both clients ~20 frames after CompleteZone; 380 frames of frames 400 gone). The flake: UpdateMusic's combat-track PickName draw followed the real-time stream thread's reported track (both installs, original and image); both games play with 'music 0' (AudioStreaming 0). 12 vanilla + 12 image runs four at a time agree, Rebirth original and image agree; follow-up SV5d6 for every other scenario. Files: `tests/scenarios/net_pair_quest.txt tests/scenarios/peer/net_pair_quest.txt tests/verify/harness_pair.h`. After: SV5d3.
- **SV10** Faster harness runs: measure where a run's wall time goes, then cut it (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-21: 'if we are running the game tests with a virtual clock, can't we run them even faster? Any downsides?' Every scenario already runs on the virtual clock (no scenario says realtime), so game time is not waited for: net_pair_quest plays about 18 minutes of game time (19,472 world updates) in about 165 s. What bounds a run is the cost of each frame: net_pair_quest's standalone run drew 6,479 frames in 165 s (about 25 ms a frame, 40 fps) at 4K with 4 games sharing the machine; startup to the menu is about 4 s. Step 1 (no golden changes): measure per frame where the time goes (render and present at 4K, the game's update, the state digest every 20 updates, the harness's checks, the GPU shared by 4-16 games, the pair's mailbox waits) on the image and the original, plus startup and shutdown. Step 2: propose cuts with their downsides for the user before changing goldens, e.g. more world updates per drawn pass (changes the interleaving of client ticks and server updates: all goldens re-recorded, the original must pace the same way), skipping draws no step reads (picking, captures and the classic shadows read what is drawn), a smaller back buffer outside capture scenarios (UI layout and click positions depend on it), cheaper digests, faster startup. Report numbers and a recommendation; implement only the cuts that keep goldens unchanged. Files: `tests/verify/harness*.cpp tests/verify/harness_clock.* tools/run_all.py tools/work_sched.py`.
- **SV10a** Pair lockstep: each game's frame step runs while the other game takes its turn (goldens unchanged by design) (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). SV10 measured: the two games of a pair never run at once (harness_pair.h: side 0's frame n+1 starts when side 1's frame n has ended), so a frame of net_pair_quest costs both games' frames: 19.3 ms median = 8-10 ms of each game's own work + 11-12 ms waiting (TIMING: pair wait), image and original alike; the pairs are the suite's longest runs (net_pair_quest bounds the suite's wall). Of a game's own frame, the drawing (app::FrameStep 00458010: the interface's Draw, EndScene, cursor, present) is 75-85%. Hand the turn on just before the frame step instead of at the after-frame peek, and wait for the next turn at the after-frame peek as now: the steps, the message pump, the worlds' updates and net::Pump stay inside the turn, so every mailbox operation and signal happens in the same order and frame as now and the goldens hold, while one game draws as the other updates. A frame then costs about max(A, B) instead of A + B: net_pair_* about 40-45% shorter (net_pair_quest ~140 s -> ~80 s at 4 games). Needs a hook point before FrameStep in the rewritten loop (app_main.cpp RunPass, ei::mainloop::g_harnessHooks) and the CALL of 00458010 in the original's WinMain redirected. Guard: a mailbox call (Bind, SendTo, RecvFrom, Close) or Signal / Signaled between the hand-off and the next turn fails the run (CInterface3D::Draw runs CloseAllPending, which can leave a game: if that ever reaches the network the guard says so). Check: the five net_pair goldens on both installs, image and --no-hooks --fresh, plain and under jitter. User 2026-10-21: do it (after SV5d5). Result (SV10a): the frame step pumps the network too (CWorldClient::Render calls net::Pump four times between its drawing stages), so the hand-off before FrameStep needed segment stamps on the datagrams (a receive takes earlier segments or its own; the frame step sends only to its own ports; other frame-step mailbox calls fail the run). Goldens unchanged. net_pair_quest median frame ~10 ms vs 18-22 ms on a quiet machine (EI_PAIR_OVERLAP=0 A/B), 76 -> 61 s in a full 16-slot suite (notes section 10). Files: `tests/verify/harness_pair.* tests/verify/harness.cpp src/game/app_main.cpp include/ei/main_loop.h`. After: SV5d5.
- **SV10b** Harness start-up: shaders compiled at build time; the start-up's DirectDraw probing and sound open measured (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). SV10 measured start-up (process start to the first frame) at 1.5-2.3 s for the image and 2.3-3.2 s for the original, about a third of the suite's game time (124 runs, median 6-7 s). Image profile of the 2.1 s: d3dcompiler compiling the renderer's shaders at every start 0.35 s (sokol's _sg_d3d11_compile_shader; compile to bytecode at build time, e.g. fxc / D3DCompile in a build step, and hand sokol the bytecode: no golden reads it, the product starts faster too), app::GetDxVersion's DirectDraw / Direct3D probing 0.45 s (the game's own check, ported; a harness answer would leave the product's code untested at start, so only with the user's word), the sound device open through AIL_waveOutOpen 0.3 s (the game's; muting it later changes nothing, not opening it changes the sounds that play and with them random draws: goldens), the exe check through imagehlp (FUN_00640060) 0.18 s. Gain of the shader part: ~0.35 s a run, ~45 s of slot time per suite. User 2026-10-21: do the faster start-up; the sound device open stays (not skipped). The GetDxVersion probe skip waits on the user (asked what it buys). SV10b done: shaders compiled at build time by fxc (src/render/shaders, render_shaders.cpp; bytecode identical to d3dcompiler's at run time but flags/checksum); the harness's FindMainThread used a machine-wide toolhelp snapshot (16,000 threads, 70-130 ms a run), now NtGetNextThread; the 9,350 hook log lines in one write; the D3D11 device made on a thread from the image's start (render::PrepareDevice). Start-up in full suites: median 3.0 -> 1.9 s, mean 2.9 -> 2.0 s. Left: GetDxVersion's probe runs through runanilla's dgVoodoo DDraw.dll (0.45-1.3 s, the most of start-up now; kept, the game's code); the sound open 0.15-0.3 s (kept). The files field is off: src/render/render_*.cpp, src/render/shaders, tests/verify/harness_clock.cpp, src/hook. Files: `src/render/render_shaders* src/render/render.cpp src/game/app_startup*`.
- **SV10c** Proposal (user decides; all goldens re-recorded): more world updates per drawn frame on the virtual clock (done 2026-10-21; opus, 2 sessions). Dropped (user 2026-10-21): don't change the number of drawn frames; make the frames faster instead (R16 renderer tickets). SV10: a frame costs 6-9 ms, of which the five world updates (server 0.2-3 ms, client 1-4 ms) are a third or less and the drawing the rest, so game time per wall second grows almost in step with the updates a frame runs: 10 instead of 5 (the virtual clock's frame-time read adds 10 ticks; RunWorlds already runs whatever backlog it gets, the original's loop too, capped by kLongestPassTicks) would cut the frames of a long scenario about in half and its in-game wall time by ~35-45% (net_pair_quest's ~6,400 frames, render_stats, the zone walks). Downsides: the client ticks and server updates interleave differently, the camera's per-pass update and the client speed balance see other steps, the client's particles draw random numbers per drawn frame: every golden changes and must be re-recorded from the original paced the same way (the harness moves the original's clock too, so it follows); scenarios' frame counts ('frames N', waits on frames) then mean more game time and some steps may land differently (re-check the scenarios that count frames). The cap kLongestPassTicks (5) must allow it in both, or the clock gives 5 and the loop is asked twice. Files: `tests/verify/harness_clock.* tests/verify/harness.cpp tests/golden`.
- **SV10d** Proposal (user decides; goldens re-recorded): draw only the frames a step reads (done 2026-10-21; opus, 2 sessions). Dropped (user 2026-10-21): don't change the number of drawn frames; make the frames faster instead (R16 renderer tickets). SV10: the drawing is 70-85% of a frame's wall time (the game's own CPU drawing: terrain projection, mesh lighting, classic shadow textures, GDI text; our renderer's submission ~6%; the GPU ~0.7 ms at 4K). Skipping the frame step (00458010 / app::FrameStep) on frames no step reads would make those frames 3-5x cheaper. But drawing changes state the digest reads: the client's particles draw ISAAC numbers while drawn (shared with the server's AI), CollectVisible sets the client units' 'model kept loaded' flag 0x400 from what is in view, the classic shadows and picking (clickunit, the cursor over units) read what was drawn, and captures need their frame. So every golden changes (re-record from the original skipping the same frames, harness-redirected CALL in WinMain), and code reached only while drawing loses coverage on skipped frames (draw every Nth frame, and always around steps that click, pick or capture). Lower priority than SV10a / SV10c. Files: `tests/verify/harness.cpp tests/verify/harness_capture.cpp tests/golden`.
- **SV10f** Image runs from a folder without dgVoodoo's DLLs, so the DirectX check takes the native path (done 2026-10-21; opus, 0.5 sessions). User 2026-10-21 ("Yeah", after: the standalone has no dgVoodoo dependency). The image (ei_standalone.exe) always draws with our D3D11 renderer, but the harness runs it from run\vanilla (and run\rebirth), the copies the original game.exe also uses, which hold dgVoodoo's DDraw.dll / D3D8.dll / D3D9.dll / D3DImm.dll. So app::GetDxVersion (0045E310) loads dgVoodoo's DDraw.dll and D3D8.dll only to read a version: 0.45-1.3 s of every image run's start-up (SV10b's profile), the largest part left. With the system's DDRAW.DLL, PF3's native path already takes DirectX 7 as given (app_dxversion.cpp). Give the image runs a run folder without dgVoodoo's DLLs (the rest of the install as now; links or a filtered copy, made by run_all's prepare like run\<install>), so the check behaves as for a player with the standalone in a clean folder. The original game.exe runs (--no-hooks) keep dgVoodoo: it draws them. Check: the log's 'native run' line in image runs, start-up before/after from the TIMING: lines (before from main's latest runs), work.py test --golden --jobs 4 0 differ on both installs. Watch for anything else the image loads from the install folder (mss32, binkw32, mp3dec.asi stay). Files: `tools/run_all.py tools/work.py src/game/app_dxversion.cpp`.
- **SV10g** launch-vanilla.cmd plays the image from a clean folder too (done 2026-10-21). SV10f moved the harness's image runs to run\vanilla-clean (no dgVoodoo DLLs, the DirectX check's native path), but launch-vanilla.cmd still starts the image with run\vanilla's game.exe, so a played session loads dgVoodoo's DDraw.dll and runs the DirectX probe. Point it at run\vanilla-clean (run_all.clean_run_dir makes it; the script can call that or link the files itself). After: SV10f.
- **SV10h** A slot is free when its game exits: frame packing and other after-game work outside the slot (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-21: fewer than 16 games run within a batch (C11's #2798). Measured from build/live/scheduler.log since 03:00: a run's slot is held on average ~5 s longer than its game (13,276 slot-seconds over 2,515 runs; median 1 s, p90 10 s; render_shadows 29 s and ui_widescreen_screens 27 s against 7-8 s games; some Rebirth runs 30-48 s). In batch #2798 the slots were full but the time with fewer than 16 slots taken was 70 of 127 s. Causes to confirm: run_all.run() packs the run's 4K frames (frame_store.pack_folder, WB11) and stores the reference after run_once() returns, inside the slot; set-up before the game (clean_run_dir for the -clean folders, saves, starter) also counts; and all 16 runner threads share one Python process (GIL), so pure-Python after-game work in one thread slows the others' set-up and reporting. Do: give the slot back when the game process ends (the 16 limit is about games on the GPU), then pack and store from a worker pool (processes, not threads, if Python work dominates; bounded so the CPU stays free for the games). Keep the results identical: a run is reported only after its packing. Measure: the lag (log time minus start minus game seconds) and games running per second over a full suite, before (this note) and after. Unit tests in tools/test_work_sched.py. The running scheduler hands over on a code change, so test on main only after merge; in the worktree run run_all / the scheduler class directly. Files: `tools/run_all.py tools/work_sched.py tools/frame_store.py`.
- **SV10i** libcrt math without a control-word switch per call when the word is already right (done 2026-10-21; sonnet, 0.5 sessions). Found by R16h (profile of render_stats' town, image): ctrlfp (the CRT's __control87_2) is 9% of the main thread's samples. MathControlWord (crt_x87.cpp) reads and sets the x87 control word around every libcrt math call (Hypot from Vector3::Length, the camera's ClampAboveObjects over every object within 16 units, frexp ...): three __control87_2 calls each. Set it only when it differs from the word wanted, restore only what was changed, and use the cheapest form (fnstcw / fldcw); the results must stay bit-identical (vectors of the libcrt family, goldens). Also check the renderer's FpuGuard (render.cpp; per mesh pass, MeshPass). Measure with renderstats' main-thread CPU per frame (R16h) in the town and the fight. Files: `src/lib/crt_x87.cpp`.
- **SV10j** std::lrint without the UCRT's FP-environment read per call (acrt_fenv_get_control 1.6% of the town) (done 2026-10-21). Found by SV10i (EI_PROFILE render_stats town, image): after the control-word fix, the UCRT's acrt_fenv_get_control is 1.6% of the main thread's samples: std::lrint (the AI's cell roundings in unit_ai_*.cpp, script_effects.cpp, combat and armour slots, world_effect_tornado ...) reads the x87 and SSE control words on every call to find the rounding mode. The original rounds with FISTP under the current x87 rounding: a shared helper (x87.h, like x87::fistp, for double and float) gives the same result without the read; check that it matches the UCRT's lrint for every rounding mode and for NaN / out-of-range values (the UCRT returns its own value and sets errno where FISTP gives 80000000), the old-against-new way SV10i did (docs/notes/standalone.md, x87 entries), plus goldens. Measure with renderstats' main-thread CPU per frame and a profile.
- **SV10k** _controlfp (006EBBB4) without __control87_2: the raw control word to the CRT's abstract form by hand (done 2026-10-21). Found by SV10j (EI_PROFILE render_stats): libcrt::Controlfp still reads (and, when it differs, sets) the word through the UCRT's __control87_2, 0.2-0.4% of the town and fight samples under terrain lighting (SectorData::Light calls it twice for every sector it lights: Controlfp(0, 0), Controlfp(0x300, 0x300), restore). FNSTCW / FLDCW plus the abstract-to-raw bit mapping (x87::ControlWord / SetControlWord) gives the same values without the call; check old against new over every control word and mask (results, control word after), as SV10i did, plus goldens.

WB16 (2026-10-20): the Rebirth install's starter\addon.log is 1.3 GB (1,305,260,835 bytes, last written 2026-10-20 15:55 while harness runs were going). Runs must never write into the game installs: send addon.dll's log writes (and any other write into an install) to the run's overlay, and add a check that a run wrote nothing in an install (log every CreateFile / MoveFile / DeleteFile for writing outside the run folder and fail the run). Don't delete or trim the existing log; that's the user's call.

## Cleanup (docs/cleanup-plan.md)

After the port and before the standalone exe. Mechanical tickets go to Sonnet (spelling only, never a signature or grouping change); type recovery and tricky debugging to Opus.

- **C28** Real virtual classes: replace the hand-built vtables (166 static Vtable(), SetVtable, slot calls) and drop layout asserts nothing needs (running since 2026-10-22; opus). User 2026-10-22: the code should look like normal game code, not fake vtable finagling. C19 compiled game.exe's tables but kept a raw table pointer at offset 0 (helper *Class templates, engine_object_class.h, mfc_slots.h), so 166 'static void* Vtable()' in 49 files and 511 SetVtable / VirtualSlot uses. Convert family by family (engine objects, managers, MFC collections, UI windows, network messages, ...) to ordinary C++ classes with virtual functions; drop offsetof/sizeof asserts unless bytes leave the process (saves, packets) or come from the data image. Each family: both suites keep their goldens (goldens are layout-independent). Split into child tickets per family. Order (survey 2026-10-22, 16 children): C28a items, C28b spells and lights, C28c party and manager context, C28d manager bases, C28e counted root / streams / links, C28f world effects, C28g worlds, C28h units / AI / map object bases, C28i data records, C28j renderer, C28k network (after C28e), C28l interface bases and the harness window ids, C28m screens and panels, C28n MFC library classes (with C14d), C28o MFC collections (with C14b), C28p retire the helpers and the asserts sweep. Method per family: C++ constructors and a virtual destructor in place of Construct / DestructBody (Construct stays as placement new where a caller builds in place), the slot bodies become the virtuals, CEngineObject's operator new / delete into the game heap.

- **C13b** Forwarders to template / alias registrations (C13 follow-up) (done 2026-10-20; opus). 1,000 forwarders whose target the map does not name as written: per-file aliases and template instantiations of the STLport / MFC container ABIs (Map8 94, Vector4 63, Str4 50, ArrayHeader<> 41, Vector12 35, PtrList 35, List16 34, CountedRefList 30, SharedRefList 27, ItemCache 21, Plain4 20 ...). Either teach tools/direct_calls.py to resolve using-aliases and instantiations against the map, or retire them with the container work (C14 / C15). Result (C13b, 2026-10-20): EI_ORIGINAL forwarders 1,617 -> 1,513 (check_cleanup). tools/direct_calls.py now resolves registrations through using-aliases (the file's own, the headers it includes, and the namespaces it opens with `using namespace`), <cstdint> typedefs and left-out default template arguments, writes template-ids through the aliases a site sees, and turns a forwarder of a static container wrapper (VectorAbi / ListAbi / TreeAbi / ArrayAbi ...) whose body is one member call into that member call on the container, also for an erased element (Opaque<N> / Bytes<N>) or a same-size integer / data pointer element (vector<unsigned int> serving vector<int> or vector<T*>, never float). Per-file strings (RawString / StringSlot / NameKey) became stlp::string in class_templates, figure_*, item_constructor, unit_save, unit_lever, world_units, world_quests, registry (mfc::CString). The six hash400 / STL ones: HashMapAt, IdMapRemoveKey, IdMapErase, IdMapLookup through hash400::PositionAt / PositionsRemoveKey / ObjectsRemoveKey / ObjectsFind (hash_tables_400.h), TreeIncrement through stlp::map::Next / rb::Next; ViewStackDestroyRange stays (CCamera::viewStack_ is camera.h's own deque layout). Of the 13 originals_library.h ones, StringAssignRange / AssignCStr / CopyConstruct / Split / Ostream* / IniGetList are gone; StringInitEmpty, StringAssignSpan, StringInitFromChars, StringErase, StringReserve remain for the net / sound callers (C13b1). Left (direct_calls --report -v, 1,516 in scope): 854 'types differ' (414 wrapper bodies that are not one member call, 189 objects of another type - 114 of them void*, 27 parameter types, 24 wrappers not inline, 22 bodies passing more than their parameters), 184 'target not found' (alias templates such as net::trees::PairTree 35, function-pointer template arguments of gamelists / gamearrays / recordarrays / mfc::impl EachElements / InitElements ...), plus the kept-on-purpose rows (Rebirth 131, skip list 94, vector recorders 49, ...). - **C16** Experiment: an SSE2 build of the image and what it changes against the goldens (done 2026-10-21; opus, 1 session). User 2026-10-21 asked about removing x87 completely; cleanup-plan Phase 4 plans an SSE2 build. Today: /arch:IA32 /fp:precise; x87:: helpers at ~770 sites (fistp 197, fsqrt 150, IsNaN 106, FromUnsigned 85, fcos 81, fsin 78, CopyFloat 36, fpatan 27, LoadStoreBits 21, flog 15, CompareStatus 10, fexp 7); the game runs the x87 at 24-bit precision in play. Experiment in a worktree, nothing merged unless it is free: build the image with /arch:SSE2 (helpers kept as they are, then a second build with the helpers written as plain C++ / SSE: lrintf for fistp, sqrtf, std::sin etc.), run the image suite, and report per golden: same / differs, the first differing frame and field, and which helper or plain-float expression caused it (bisect by turning helpers back one family at a time). Expected: float arithmetic at 24-bit precision matches SSE single except exponent range; doubles computed under PC24, the transcendental instructions (fsin, fcos, fpatan, flog, fexp) and fistp's rounding mode are where results can part. Output: a table of families that are free to convert (goldens the same), families that change results and how much, and a recommendation for keeping an oracle (bit-exact x87-emulating helpers vs re-recorded goldens with behaviour-level checks). Frame-time difference too (main-thread CPU in render_stats). Files: `CMakeLists.txt src/lib/x87* docs/cleanup-plan.md`.
- **C16a** x87:: maths helpers to std:: at the call sites (C16 follow-up) (done 2026-10-21). C16 made x87::fsqrt a plain wrapper of std::sqrt (FSQRT at the precision control on this target; goldens the same, docs/notes/cleanup-tooling.md 'SSE2 build'). Replace its call sites with std::sqrt and drop the wrapper. Keep the double argument: where the argument is a float expression write std::sqrt(static_cast<double>(x)) (std::sqrt(float) is sqrtf, another function on 53-bit threads). fsin / fcos / fpatan / flog / flog10 stay instructions: C16's commit 1faab7c4 (std:: maths, goldens the same in the suite but not provably bit-exact: the CRT gives 53-bit results where the instructions give 64) was left out of main. x87::fistp stays (lrint reads the FP environment per call, SV10j) and x87::fexp stays (VC6's inline exp parts the goldens). Mechanical: Sonnet; goldens and ei_vectors must stay the same. After: C16.

User wishlist 2026-10-20: the standalone game loads (and saves) its settings from a file instead of HKCU\Software\Nival Interactive\EvilIslands. Import the registry values once if the file is missing. A file per run would also give the harness its pinned settings for free (WB16). User 2026-10-21: go. The file lives next to the exe; standalone only (game.exe in the hook build keeps the registry). Shape: a settings file behind win_registry.cpp's CWinRegistry / helpers (call sites barely change), one-time import from HKCU\Software\Nival Interactive\EvilIslands when the file is missing, the ~7 direct registry callers (console_addon, mod_folders, script_addon_commands, system_text, ui_widescreen, world_client_draw, hook/paths) switched over, the harness writing a pinned file per standalone run (original-suite game.exe runs keep the registry). Opus. Done 2026-10-22: src/game/settings_file.{h,cpp}, ei_settings.ini next to ei_standalone.exe (sections = key paths below HKCU, reg-export value syntax plus float:), imported once from HKCU Nival Interactive\EvilIslands and Gipat.Ru\EI_Starter when missing; CWinRegistry and the 5 direct readers use it under ei::Standalone(); paths.cpp / discprompt are read filters; the harness pins <run folder>\ei_settings.ini from registry.reg + starter lines (read only). ## Lua scripting (docs/notes/lua-scripting.md)

After the standalone exe (user 2026-10-14). Mission scripts run on Lua through a transpiler (assets unchanged), mods can write Lua, saves keep running coroutines (Eris).

- **L1** Script IR from the existing parser (later). Blocks, commands, definitions, globals, Rebirth's extensions, with a text dump; every mission, quest and console form parses. After: S.
- **L2** Vendor Lua and generate the bindings (later). Decide Lua 5.3 + Eris or Luau + Ares; bindings from the keyword table (300 + Rebirth's 69); object references and groups as userdata. After: L1.
- **L3** Transpiler to resumable Lua functions (later). Blocks as block(pc, run), rule sets, For / ForIf bodies (a wait ends the body), assignments, script calls, the WorldScript. After: L2.
- **L4** Scheduler with ScriptTick's exact order (later). Head insertion, same-tick vs next-tick, Execute count, Sleep ticks, NaN as false. After: L3.
- **L5** Lockstep luaboth verification (later). Both engines per tick: variables, run positions, instances, the built-in call log; scenarios plus a mission corpus. After: L4.
- **L6** Saves through Eris; old saves as coroutines (later). Permanents, __persist by object id; decide whether to keep writing the old format. After: L5.
- **L7** Quests and console lines through the transpiler (later). QFinish's text and console lines at runtime; a cache for mission files. After: L4.
- **L8** Mod API (later). .lua loading, Lua names for the built-ins, waits as yields, sandbox, errors as "Script error". After: L6.
- **L9** Lua by default; old executor removed (later). The parser stays as the front end. After: L5, L6, L7, L8.

Finished 2026-10-14: every hookable game function is rewritten (99.97% of game code); 201 stubs of 1-4 bytes wait for the standalone build. Area history is in the area notes.

- **A-Z1** The last eight functions: the port is complete (done 2026-10-14). The last eight functions ported: the port is complete (function_sizes.inc hand-patched for two sizes).
- **A-I23** Interface functions no scenario checks (done 2026-10-21). The connection screen Create / list draw, exchange slots 0062D100 / 0062D200. Also unreached: WinMain's second-instance, disc prompt and frame-recording branches, 0041ECC0, 00443080, CDib's catch handlers. Done (A-I23): golden now: Create 0061B2C0 / list draw 0061D780 / 00443080 (every pair's join), exchange slots 0062D100 / 0062D200 (net_pair_zone), 0041ECC0 + 005813A0 (net_pair_leave: the vanilla peer now hides z7q2.mq, vanilla never offered z6q1; samefile checks the written file), WinMain's frame-recording branch (app_record), the disc prompt (app_disc_prompt: Rebirth original golden; the vanilla install's game.exe has EB at 00456BB5 and never asks, image golden there). Not from a scenario: the second instance (exit -1 in WinMain before any step, no state for a golden), CDib's catch handlers (need a CFileException from a failing disk; the box ends the run), the DirectX 7 check and the low-disk box; a vector test does not fit (WinMain's calls, MFC's TRY frames: verify_mfc_try.cpp drove them before S21d). Coverage: coverage.py addresses / collect on the original with EI_COVER (perturbs real-time threads: not for goldens).
- **PF1** Script keyword matching without per-character locale calls (done 2026-10-14; opus, 0.5 sessions). Script keyword matching (ClassifyToken 004CF600) uses a 256-byte case-fold table built from the game CRT's tolower instead of per-character _strnicmp locale calls; no behaviour change. Files: `src/game/script_parse.cpp`.
- **PF1a** Case-fold table for the other game _strnicmp callers (done 2026-10-14; sonnet, 0.3 sessions). Other game _strnicmp callers (CMappedResource::FindEntry, object3d_figure_setup.cpp, net_server_players.cpp) use SameIgnoringCase / CaseFoldTable from lib/crt where hot. Files: `src/game/resource.cpp`, `src/game/object3d_figure_setup.cpp`, `src/game/net_server_players.cpp`. After: PF1.
- **A-I23a** Scenario: a texture from a loose .bmp (CTexture::LoadBmp, CDib::Read) (done 2026-10-21). CTextureManager::LoadTexture 006922A0 loads <data>textures\<name>.bmp (with a_<name>.bmp as alpha) when the file exists: LoadBmp 00690260 -> LoadBmpFiles 0068FBF0 -> CDib::Construct 006D4AA0, Read 006D5910, Size 006D4D10, MakePalette 006D54D0, all unreached (no install has a loose .bmp). A '# prepare:' tool writing a 24-bit .bmp and an 8-bit alpha for a texture zone1 loads, into the run's overlay, reaches them; golden from the original, a capture compared on the image. After: A-I23.
